# Assumptions and wording for Josh

The builder records here every choice the spec, decisions and mockups did not settle, and any user-visible text not on a mockup. Josh confirms or changes them in bulk; a change becomes a new ticket.

| EZD-397 | Why EZD-117a B2 (warm Projects page, first cards within 1,000 ms) took 1033.2 ms and 1013.1 ms on CI, and the fix | Not an app defect: between the last green run (6229456b) and the first red one (d1db60f0) only editor, geometry, icon and catalog files changed, and locally the warm first card shows at 75 to 106 ms (4 runs of a throwaway timing spec, idle machine). The extra time is other browser workers' load on the CI runner. Fix: B2 moved unchanged (same title, bound 1,000 ms, no retry) to app/e2e/perf-projects-warm.spec.ts, so the `perf` project runs it alone after the device projects. Side effect: like every perf spec, B2 is skipped when a device-project test fails, and the gate fails then anyway. | 11 Budgets "Projects page"; EZD-207c D-Isolate (docs/architecture.md "Perf project"); git diff 6229456b..d1db60f0 |

| EZD-421 | Which failures make the route check whether the session ended | Only session-loss forms: PostgREST 42501, PGRST301, PGRST303; Auth AuthSessionMissingError or codes session_not_found, session_expired, bad_jwt, refresh_token_not_found, refresh_token_already_used; also one level of `cause`. Every other failure keeps its 500 and alert without the check. Storage API errors are not a session-loss form | Q-0052 (c); plan Part a "names the class, not the 42501 example" |
| EZD-421 | How "ended" is confirmed | A fresh session client (not getSession's cache): ended when the cookies hold no session, Auth refuses the session with a 4xx (seen on the local stack: a refused refresh answers AuthApiError 400 refresh_token_not_found and clears the cookies), the verified exp has passed, or current_app_user_id() returns null. An Auth 5xx or network error, a failed lookup, or the check throwing keeps the 500 and alert | Q-0052 (c); EZD-395; L-059 |
| EZD-421 | Modules that wrap a database error in a new Error (user-key rpcFailure, assistant slot, throttle, source-files) | They keep the original error as `cause` (reports still carry only the error name, EZD-169); readMyKey's 'denied' 42501 is kept as `{ code: '42501' }`. password.ts rethrows an ended-session error from the throttle and from the cookie session's updateUser / reauthenticate so the route answers 401; changePassword's own in-memory sign-in session is not the cookie session and is unchanged | EZD-169; vault/plans/EZD-421.md Part b |

| EZD-223 | When a stroke counts as a line, arc, circle or rectangle | Closed when the ends are within 20% of the stroke length. Line: no point more than 6% of the chord off it. Arc or circle: no point more than 12% of the radius off the fitted circle, and an arc bends at least 8% of its chord. Rectangle: four corners each within 20° of square. Anything else stays raw. Shares of the stroke's own size, so zoom does not change the result | S03 Sketch |
| EZD-223 | Pencil only and the rest of the plan | Pencil only applies to Sketch: a touch never draws a sketch, while two-finger pan and pinch zoom still work; other tools keep their touch behaviour. A touch that lands during a Pencil stroke (a palm) is ignored | S03 Sketch; S15d iPad input mode |

The measure is not saved and nothing else is | 04 Tool rail "Distance between two points; no model change" |

A click, Shift-click, drag or box on any member selects the whole group; a member put in a new group leaves its old one, and a group left with one member goes. When a selected object is in a group the menu shows Ungroup instead of Group. A deleted member reads as gone; the group itself stays | S03 Context menu; 03f Objects inside model_json; S03 "Repeated click cycles outward" |

| EZD-302 | Which layers are walls, doors, windows and rooms in a Polycam DXF | Real Polycam layer names are unknown (no real file yet). A layer takes the role its name contains, ignoring case: "wall", "door", "window", and "room", "space" or "floor". The detector also takes an explicit layer map, which EZD-306 can fill for a DXF that is not from Polycam. Check against the real file once supplied | 08 Imports "detected by layer"; fixtures/README.md "Real Polycam layer names are unknown"; phase-03 EZD-306 |
| EZD-302 | How confidence is scored (0 to 100, shown as %) | Wall: 95 when two parallel faces 1 to 12 in apart are found, 60 for a single line; 25 off for each end that does not reach the corner it shares with another wall within the tolerance. Door: 90 with its leaf line, 75 arc only. Window: 90 for two or more parallel lines, 75 for one. 20 off an opening with no host wall. Room: 90 closed polyline, 60 open. The high-confidence cutoff that sorts these into To check stays a company setting (EZD-308) | S02 Data shown "confidence %", Validation; Round 24 cutoff in Defaults |
| EZD-302 | Join tolerance | 0.5 in (08 "About 0.5" tolerance"; S02 mockup 1/2"). There is no Defaults tolerance setting in code yet; the detector takes it as an option, so the company value can be passed in once it exists | 08 Imports; S02 Data shown "Tolerance from company settings under Defaults" |
| EZD-302 | Units of a DXF with no or unitless $INSUNITS | Read as inches. Feet, mm, cm and m are converted | 03 rule 5 (decimal inches) |
| EZD-302 | Which wall face is the wall line | The face nearer the middle of the detected rooms (or of all wall lines when there is no room) is the inside face; start to end runs with the thickness on the right | EZD-202 Wall line "start to end is the inside finished face" |
| EZD-302 | The unjoined-wall fixture (Q-0066 (b)) | The synthetic room with the east wall's two face lines stopped 2 in short of the north wall (4 times the 1/2" tolerance). The east wall loses one joined end; the north wall still reaches the corner point, so only the east wall scores lower | Ticket Done when; Q-0066 (b); fixtures/synthetic/room-12x10-unjoined.dxf.expected.json |

Members only read sessions for now (standard grant, no write policy, so RLS refuses member writes); review actions get their write policies in EZD-308/309 | 03c import_session; S02 Mode "Default shown in mockup"; S02 States Error |

| EZD-303 | How GLB axes map to the plan | glTF metres, +Y up (Khronos glTF 2.0); plan x = X, plan y (north) = -Z, height = Y above the floor, ÷ 0.0254 to inches. This matches the synthetic fixture; a real Polycam GLB may face another way (fixtures/README.md), so it is checked again when the real file arrives | Khronos glTF 2.0 Coordinate system and units; fixtures/README.md GLB; manifest polycam-glb "not yet supplied" |
| EZD-303 | Which mesh surfaces are the ceiling | Triangles whose front-face (counter-clockwise) normal points within 60° of straight down; the floor level is the lowest up-facing triangle. Pitch and slope come from a best-fit plane through the ceiling; the low wall is the downhill side, the high wall the uphill side; a flat ceiling has pitch 0 and no low or high wall | S02 Decisions "heights at points (low/high wall), pitch, or the value read from the mesh"; S05 pitch as n/12 |

| EZD-411 | What the outline is | The top as cut, overhangs included; `overhang` on each edge records how far it stands past the cabinet face. An item set back to unresolved (undo) has its row removed. The mode is saved on import_session.mode. Anything unsent goes out at once (keepalive) when the page hides | phase-03 EZD-309; 00d glossary Outbox (2 s, one save at a time) |

| EZD-309 | What a fix is and what it does | Fix selected toggles Fix mode on the selected item. Drag handles: a wall's two ends, an opening's two edges (they move along the host wall; at least 1 in wide), a room's corners. Each finished drag is one action. It accepts the item and stores its corrected geometry. Scan reads still shows the detected value. Changing a door swing or an item's type is not offered: detection gives no swing, and type changes wait for a later ticket. Esc cancels a drag in progress and leaves Fix mode; finished drags stay (undo removes them) | S02 Controls Fix selected, Drag endpoint / edge, Esc; Decisions "resolved (accepted, fixed or deleted)" |
| EZD-309 | Snaps in review | The Plan editor's snap (EZD-206) runs against the review's other walls: endpoints, midpoints and faces within 10 px, then 1/2" increments, plus angle from the wall's other end for a wall end | S02 "Snaps as in Plan editor"; 09j Snapping |
| EZD-309 | Delete, selection sync and undo | Delete marks the item rejected, same as Reject. It is not drawn and can be undone. One by one then moves to the next item; the other modes move to the next item to check. A list click pans the canvas when the item is off screen; a canvas click selects the topmost item (openings over walls over rooms). Ctrl/Cmd+Z and Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z undo and redo decisions and fixes for this session; mode changes are not undone | S02 Controls Delete, To check list row, Click item on canvas, Ctrl/Cmd+Z |

| EZD-220 | What "the Phase eyes override" means for pins (AC-EZD-220-5) | A pin is drawn, picked and snapped only in the Existing view, and there the Existing eye still decides (off hides pins). In Proposed pins are hidden whatever the eyes say, since the Existing eye is on by default there. The rule is a type test in the layer setting's display filter (app/src/editor/plan/phase-filter.ts), so draw, pick and hover all follow it | Q-0704 05-screens; S03 Decisions "The Phase eye toggles override the display"; AC-EZD-220-5 |
| EZD-220 | Where pins are placed and how they look on the plan | Pins are a Pins section in the Fixtures panel (F), one button per kind; a click places one, always Existing (even in Proposed, where it is then hidden, which the section's hint says). The plan mark is a map pin: a 6" radius circle whose centre is 10" above the point and a 4" stem down to the point, on the Existing layer, drawn after the geometry drawing list (packages/geometry drawing build is held by EZD-422). Boards are waived (Q-0705) | S03 Fixtures; W3; Q-0704 05-screens; Q-0705 |
| EZD-220 | Photo attach and its failure | The photo is attached from the pin's inspector (one pin selected). The pin is placed with no photo; the file is started as source_file kind photo, uploaded and completed, and only then does the pin take the id (so the save check never sees an unfinished upload). A failure keeps the pin and shows Retry, which sends only the part left (start, bytes or complete). Offline the upload fails and shows Retry; the pin itself queues in the outbox. The thumbnail loads through GET /api/source-files/{id}/link, which needs only See projects, reads the row through the caller's client and signs only finished `photo` files (so it never opens a cost file) | Q-0704 05-screens, 03-data-model row 3, 10-permissions; 03c:15 |
| EZD-220 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on a board) | Fixtures panel: "Existing-condition pins", "Pins show in the Existing view only."; kinds "Damage / moisture", "Protect", "Lead", "Plumbing", "Gas", "Water heater"; inspector: "{kind} pin", "Kind", "Photo", "No photo yet.", "Loading photo…", "Photo attached. This browser can't show it." (a HEIC the browser cannot draw, as outside Safari), "{kind} photo" (image text), "Attach photo", "Replace photo", "Uploading photo…", "Photo not uploaded. The pin is kept.", "Retry", "Choose a JPEG, PNG or HEIC photo.", "This photo is over 50 MB.", "This file is empty." | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |

| EZD-310 | Tolerance while the company's Defaults value is Not set | 1/2". The review reads the effective measurement_tolerance (the one Defaults row that covers field measurements and scan-vs-typed, Round 24 #30); Not set falls back to 1/2", the S02 text value. Board S02's 1/8" footer is sample data. A difference exactly at the tolerance is no conflict ("greater than") | S02 Data shown "(mockup: 1/2")", Validation; R24 #30; Defaults row measurement_tolerance (EZD-116) |
| EZD-310 | Where a typed value is kept and how it changes the item | In the item's fix (`typed`, inches), saved per item like any EZD-309 fix, so undo, reload and Finish need nothing new and no new table. A typed wall keeps its start and its end moves along the wall to the typed length; a typed opening keeps its centre and takes the typed width. Typing accepts the item, as a fix does; a later drag replaces the fix and drops the typed value; emptying the field removes the fix (the item stays accepted, back at the scan). Rooms have no typed field (Scan reads shows their area) | S02 Typed or laser input "it overrides the scan"; Decisions "resolved (accepted, fixed or deleted)"; Q-0215 |
| EZD-310 | How the conflicts a typed value creates are worked out (the room walk) | A closed room's boundary walls (both ends and the middle on its boundary) are walked in boundary order from the room's first corner, which stays put. A typed length stretches its wall from where the walk enters it, and every later wall moves with it; where the walk misses the first corner by more than the tolerance is the gap (red error "Room no longer closes: {size} gap at the {corner} corner", the corner named by compass against the middle of the room's corners). Openings on the typed wall change their offset from its far corner by the full change; openings on later walls move along their wall by the part of the change that runs along it; amounts under 1/32" are not listed (amber warning "{opening} offset from corner changes by {amount}"). A typed opening width moves each edge by half the change (one warning on itself). This puts the gap away from the typed wall and warns on openings of other walls, as board S02 shows (north wall typed, gap at the southwest corner). Errors list first, then warnings in detection order. Amounts show to 1/16" | S02 Conflict list, Validation "Room closure: typed lengths must add up", "moves an opening relative to a corner"; board S02 |
| EZD-310 | What else shows with the list | A Conflict pill beside the item name when the typed value is beyond the tolerance; the red gap marker with its size on the canvas at the gap corner (S02 Data shown). Clicking a line centres the canvas on it at 400% (at least). The warning icon is the existing `report` icon in amber (the icon set has no triangle). Not built here: the "typed · scan" chip on the canvas, the "change in Defaults" link (Conflict threshold link, needs Manage users and settings) and Resolve the gap (EZD-311) | S02 Controls; board S02; Q-0500 |
| EZD-310 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on a board) | "{size} gap" (canvas marker); "Tolerance {value} · shared with field measurements" now shows the company value; the length refusal reuses "Use 12'6", 12' 6 1/2", 150" or 12.5'" | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

| EZD-311 | Finish import before EZD-313 builds it | Finish stays disabled (EZD-307 assumption). Any boundary wall of that room may be picked; a wall of no room or of another room is refused with "Pick a wall of this room"; a wall across the gap (what would be left across it is over the tolerance) with the Plan editor's "That wall runs across the gap..."; under 1/32" or over 200' 0" with the Plan editor's length refusals. A gap with parts both ways offers Keep only, with the Plan editor's "This gap runs both ways. Move a wall to close it." (Fix moves a wall here). It resolves the gap only while the walk still misses by that vector (within 1/32"); a later typed value that changes the gap opens it again, as closureOverride.gap does in the Plan editor. Kept gaps show no red marker and no gap line in the conflict list. Dragging a kept room's corner keeps it | S02 Keep "Records an override"; Q-0570; EZD-210a keepGap |

Pick mode: Esc goes back to the section; a list click leaves it. The section is the Plan editor's (editor/inspector/ResolveGap.tsx), so no new wording. The review page has no Edit model or lock state yet; hiding the buttons without Edit model (Q-0570) comes with EZD-314's permission and lock states (the server already refuses the save) | Q-0630; Q-0570; S02 Esc; EZD-309 assumption on the edit lock |

## EZD-313 (2026-11-17)

| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |

| EZD-313 | Where the objects Finish writes come from | The server builds them from what the import has saved (decisions and fixes), never from the browser. The page first sends what its save queue still holds, then POSTs finish. Every accepted item at its fixed position becomes a new model object with status Existing on the project's first floor (lowest floor order). Deleted items are left out. finish_import checks again that every detected item has a decision, writes the objects, closes the import's sessions and makes one checkpoint (the new model version), all in one transaction | S02 Finish import; W2 step 5; S02 Decisions "Nothing imports with a pending flag" |
| EZD-313 | Fields detection does not give | Walls: the detected thickness as drywall 1/2" + stud + drywall 1/2" (a whole stud layer when 1" or less; the default build when none is detected), default height 96", thickness to the right, not bearing. Doors and windows: the detected width; the default height (door 80", window 48"), automatic rough opening, window sill 36", door swing and hand left, not impact rated. A door or window keeps its host when its host wall is imported too, otherwise it is off a wall (flagged in Plan). Rooms are named "Room N" after the project's highest Room number; a room kept out of square carries its closure override (who, when, gap). Numbers are given by the numbering trigger | packages/geometry createWallData, createOpeningData defaults; room naming as rooms/sync.ts |
| EZD-313 | Cancel import permission, lock and what is discarded | Cancel needs Edit model and is refused (409) while another user holds the edit lock, like every other review change. It closes the import's sessions (cancelled) and removes the saved review rows. The detected items stay in the closed session row (no longer shown or editable). No model object, model version or source file is touched. Cancel stays available while detecting or failed; with no session rows yet it closes nothing | S02 Cancel import; Q-0215; EZD-309 lock assumption |

| EZD-313 | How Plan gets the counts | Finish opens /projects/[id]?imported=walls.windows.doors.rooms; Plan shows one green toast and takes the query off the address. Offline Finish (queued as an offline change) is not built here | S02 Decisions "opens 03 Plan with a short notice of the counts imported"; S02 States Offline |

Nothing was imported.", "Open the plan". Errors: "Couldn't finish that. Try again.", "This import is not ready to finish yet.", "Resolve every item and room gap before you finish the import." | EZD-215 | The letter | A PDF, JPEG, PNG or HEIC file sent as source_file kind engineer_letter (new kind) through the same upload as a pin photo; the note takes its id as soon as it is uploaded. Save writes the name, the approval text and the tick | Q-0704 row 3; phase-02.md:33 |

| EZD-215 | Editor download limit | The rule runs inside every edit, so it is in the editor's first download; with it the editor route measured 501,036 compressed bytes against the 500,000 limit. To make room, the confirm dialog and its Radix AlertDialog (about 40 KB minified) now load when the first confirm is asked (lazy/confirm-dialog), as other panels do; the route measures 486,789 bytes. The code is fetched on the first press or key on the page, so it is there before a confirm is asked; if it still cannot load, the confirm is answered Cancel, so nothing destructive runs. The limit is unchanged; EZD-423 still owns freeing its 25,000 bytes | S11 Download size; 11 rule 7; EZD-423 |
| EZD-423 | What moves out of the editor entry | The drawing engine (every drawing tool's code) loads as lazy/drawing-engine on the first pointer or key input in the page, as the overlay and the lock UI already do; `@ezdraft/geometry` is marked side-effect free so unused geometry leaves the entry. The drawing layer and typed bar stay in the entry, so typing works from the first key. zod stays: object checks run at load (room areas, model-derived). The editor route measures 472,714 compressed bytes (was 486,912) | S11 Download size; 11 rule 7; AC-EZD-423-1, -2 |
| EZD-423 | Input made before the engine is there | A tool key or rail press shows the tool at once; the plan's presses, typed values, Esc and Enter in the meantime are held and handed to the engine in order, so the first click of a wall chain, a typed size or the first drag is not lost. Until it loads, a press on a selected wall's handle or door arc goes to the selection (the engine is fetched on that page's first input, so this only happens if the very first press is on a handle) | AC-EZD-423-2 |
| EZD-423 | How AC-EZD-423-1's 25,000 bytes are proven | Measured once at this ticket's gate: the new editor-drawing-lazy A1 test prints the editor route's size and the commit records it. The lasting test keeps the 500,000-byte limit (as B11 and EZD-207a B8 do) and checks that the engine and layer files stay out of the entry. A lasting 475,000 limit would block the tickets the room was made for (EZD-410, EZD-422) | AC-EZD-423-1; ticket title "so tickets have room" |
| EZD-314 | What "item count so far" counts while detection runs | The items of the files already read: the import job writes one session per file as it reads it, so a two-file import shows the first file's items while the second is read. One file shows "Detecting items…" until it is done (detection of one file is one step) | S02 States Loading; server/jobs/handlers/import.ts |
| EZD-314 | Who may Retry a failed import, and what it does | Edit model, as for Cancel import; refused while another user holds the edit lock. Retry removes the failed sessions and puts the same job back on the queue as new (no attempts, no error); the page then reads the import again until detection ends. Uploaded files are not touched | S02 States Error, Permissions; EZD-313 Cancel rule |
| EZD-314 | When a project card shows "Importing" | While an import job of the project is queued or running, or a review is open (a session of that job detecting or ready, not yet finished or cancelled). A failed import, or a session whose job was removed, does not show Importing. The open-items count is a separate project field and is not changed by imports | S01 States "Import in progress"; Q-0215 (items are kept until Finish or Cancel) |
| EZD-314 | Where a bearing flag comes from | A detected wall item may carry `bearing: true`; no detector sets it yet (Polycam and DXF files carry no bearing data), so the flag is shown and carried to the model when a source does. Marking a wall bearing during review is not built (no control on the board); it is set in Plan after Finish (EZD-213) | S02 Validation; board S02 |
| EZD-314 | Locked state | Import review never takes the edit lock; the review is locked while another user holds it (save_import_review refuses only then). The editor's lock banner is reused under the header; Finish, Cancel, Retry and every review action are disabled | S02 States Locked / view-only; EZD-126 LockBanner |
| EZD-314 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on a mockup) | "Detecting items… {count} items found so far" (one: "{count} item"); "Retry" / "Retrying…"; "Couldn't start the retry. Try again."; "This import hasn't failed, so there's nothing to retry."; flag "Bearing"; card "Importing" and the note "You can view this import but not change it" are the spec's words | CLAUDE.md rule 6; S02 States; S01 States |
| EZD-314 | Empty state button (S02 States Empty "a button to open the Plan editor with the underlay") | A primary button "Open the Plan editor" under the message; it opens the project in Plan, which shows the PDF underlay (EZD-304). Wording for Josh to confirm | S02 States Empty; EZD-305 row |
| EZD-314 | Detecting state "canvas shows underlay only" | Not drawn: while detecting the page shows the message with the count and no canvas (the underlay comes with the review once detection ends) | S02 States Loading |

## EZD-305 (2026-11-18)

| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EZD-305 | Which PDF lines are walls (a PDF has no layers) | The straight stroked lines of the page's heaviest line weight: every stroke at least 75% as wide as the widest one (plans draw walls with the heaviest pen). Filled shapes, curves, text and images are not read. The lines then go through EZD-302's wall detector as one wall layer, so face pairing, end joins and confidence are the DXF import's | S01 PDF: detect type; fixture room-12x10-vector.pdf (walls 1.5 pt, other lines 0.5 pt) |
| EZD-305 | What is read from a vector PDF | Walls only. Doors, windows, rooms and supplier cabinet labels are not read, so a wall broken by a door or window gap comes in as separate wall pieces (the fixture's south wall reads as 3 pieces) and is fixed in review. A supplier PDF (kind supplier_pdf) is not read here | phase-03 EZD-305 "Auto-detect wall lines"; S02 States Supplier kitchen PDF (later ticket) |
| EZD-305 | Which page, and at what scale | The calibrated page (EZD-304 scale), placed as the underlay is (first calibration point at the plan origin, page right east, page up north), so detected walls sit on the underlay. Without a scale (Q-0203 never blocks): page 1 at 1/4" = 1'-0" from the page's lower-left corner | Q-0203; 09 Sheet set rules plan scale; EZD-304 underlay placement |
| EZD-305 | Scanned PDF | A page with no stroked lines (a scanned page is one image) gives a ready session with no items, which Import review shows as its empty state (EZD-314). A file pdf.js cannot open gives a failed session, code unreadable_pdf (S02 Error with file name and Retry) | S02 States Empty, Error |
| EZD-305 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on a mockup) | Import review status bar source for a PDF import: "PDF" (the board shows "Polycam Space · DXF + GLB" for a scan) | CLAUDE.md rule 6; S02 status bar |
| EZD-405 | How a side-edge drag steps and what code a resized box gets | Whole 3" steps, the nearest step to the drag; each step lands on the 3" grid, so a custom 16 1/2" box goes to 18" or 15"; never under 3". The code's number is swapped when it starts with the old width (B15 to B18, W1530 to W1830, F3 to F6); a code that does not carry its width is kept. A narrowed filler's scribe is cut to its width | S04 Controls "Drag box side edge"; S04 Validation; assumptions.md EZD-401 seed codes |
| EZD-405 | Where the edges and drags are | Edge handles are thin strips inside each side of a box in the elevation (the plan strip's uppers lie over the base boxes); dragging a box body in either view reorders it in its own row, placed after every other box whose middle is left of the pointer; the run shows the result live and the edit is one undo step when the pointer is let go | S04 Controls "Drag box", "Drag box side edge"; AC-EZD-404-1 |
| EZD-405 | Double-click, Swap box and Save as group before EZD-406 | Double-click opens the typed width entry under the run header (the inspector's Width field comes with EZD-406). The context menu has Duplicate and Delete; Swap box waits for EZD-406's compatible list and Save as group for the Saved library | S04 Controls; phase-04 EZD-406 row |
| EZD-405 | Typed height dimensions | Upper sets every upper box's height; Base is the countertop top, so the counter-height base boxes take it less the 1 1/2" countertop (tall boxes keep theirs). The 18" gap is not stored (EZD-404) and is not typable | S04 "Height dimension text"; assumptions.md EZD-404 heights |
| EZD-405 | Undo, redo and Delete on the cabinet screen | Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z and Delete / Backspace act on the model's one undo stack and the cabinet selection only, with Edit model and the lock; Esc cancels a drag, then closes the menu or entry, then clears the selection | S04 Controls "Delete / Backspace", "Esc", "Ctrl/Cmd+Z" |
| EZD-405 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on a mockup) | Entry labels "B15 Width" (the box's code and "Width"), "Base height", "Upper height"; refusal "Type a size above 0, e.g. 18 or 1' 6""; menu "Box actions" with "Duplicate", "Delete"; edge tooltip "Drag to resize" | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |
| EZD-422 | Proportions of each symbol | Fractions of the fixture's width and depth so a symbol keeps its look at any size: toilet tank the back 30% of the depth, full width, bowl an oval in the front 70%, 80% of the width; lavatory oval basin 70% × 60%; tub basin inset 10% of the smaller side with rounded corners, drain at the end along the axis's minus side; sink two bowls split at the center line with a deck 20% of the depth at the back and a drain in each; burners radius 18% of the smaller side at a quarter of the width and depth from the center; drain marks 3" across. Ovals and rounded corners are drawn as short lines | AC-EZD-422-1; Q-0705 standard drafting marks |
| EZD-422 | Prefab shower "a square with both diagonals" vs "No kind draws the outline with both diagonals" | The outline, the pan's lip inset 6% of the smaller side, the lip's two diagonals and a center drain circle; no line joins the outline's corners | AC-EZD-422-1 |
| EZD-422 | Cooktop vs range | The cooktop is the four burners only (the countertop draws around it); the range is its outline and the four burners | AC-EZD-422-1 "range also its outline to the floor" |
| EZD-422 | Hood "dashed outline" | Drawn as 3" dashes with 2" gaps in the same 'beyond' line weight as the other fixtures (AC-EZD-422-2 keeps the line weight; the plan canvas does not dash the 'hidden' role) | AC-EZD-422-1, -2 |
| EZD-422 | Letters REF, DW, GD, W, D, WH | DM Mono at the dimension text size, centered on the fixture, along its axis and turned so they never read upside down; they draw in the drawing text colour, as dimension text does | AC-EZD-422-1; S03 Text |
| EZD-410 | How "left off a wall after a wall move" is known | Read from the model each time, never stored: the run keeps its host wall id (unlike an opening, whose host is cleared), and the screen flags it when its start is not on the wall's line or either face (1/64"), its axis does not run along the wall, or its rows share no length with the wall; also when its host wall is gone or on another floor. A run with no host is a freestanding (island) run and is never flagged, since a wall move never clears a host. Undo, a move back or a wall delete set or clear it with no extra edit. A run longer or shorter than its wall stays on it (that is the fit chip's mismatch, EZD-407) | S04 Validation "Hosted cabinet left off a wall after a wall move: red 'Not on a wall' flag"; walls/move.ts "hosted items do not move"; 03g 1/64" |
| EZD-410 | Where the red flag shows | On the cabinet run screen: a red "Not on a wall" flag with the error icon in the run header (as the 03 inspector's opening flag), and every box of the run outlined red in the plan strip and the elevation. Cabinets are not drawn on the 03 plan and have no inspector yet, so nothing is added there | S04 Validation; S04 Layout; AC-EZD-410-1 |
| EZD-410 | Wording | "Not on a wall" is spec text (S04 Validation) and the same words as the opening flag; no hint line is added | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |

No existing feature is wired in yet (sync conflicts, renders and the rest register in their own tickets) | 04 Header card row; phase-01 EZD-127 |

Try again." with "Retry"; "Nothing to review" | CLAUDE.md rule 6; 04 Header card row |

| EZD-505 | How count and spacing lay out the set | Spacing = span ÷ count; the first and last beams are half a spacing from the walls. From a typed spacing: count = floor(span ÷ spacing), with equal end gaps (span − (count−1) × spacing) ÷ 2. Positions stored to 1/16" (03g halfway rule). Worked example: span 20' 0", 5 beams → 4' 0" o.c., end gaps 2' 0". This matches the board: a 20' × 16' room at 23 px/ft, beams at 2', 6', 10', 14', 18' | board S05 SVG (room 60–520 px; beam centres 106…474 px); Q-0207 |
| EZD-505 | Length "wall to wall" in a room that is not a rectangle | Each beam's own inside wall-to-wall distance along its line. A typed Length applies to all beams, centred on the room's middle line across the set | S05 Length row |
| EZD-505 | Direction with no ridge (flat, tray, coffered) | "With ridge" = parallel to the room's longest wall, "Across ridge" = across it; board labels kept | S05 Direction row |
| EZD-505 | Which Placement options a ceiling type allows | Flat, tray, coffered: only "On flat ceiling". Single slope and vaulted: all three, default "Level beams in vault" | S05 Placement; 03-data-model.md#Q-0664 |

Its finished ceiling height is its flat height (EZD-501 default: wall height), or its lowest ceiling point when not flat. With no adjoining room, Match is fixed to Custom | S05 Match row; EZD-501 row |
| EZD-505 | "Below the ridge and at or above the plate" | Warn when the beam top (Height AFF + H) is above the ridge height, or Height AFF is below the wall plate (the lowest wall height under the ceiling). Exactly at either is fine. Non-blocking | S05 Validation; AC-EZD-505-1 |
| EZD-505 | Moving one beam alone | Click selects the set, a second click that beam alone, a third the ceiling. Dragging a lone beam takes it out as a set of one; the rest keep their spacing with count − 1 | S05 drag row; 05-screens.md#Q-0630 |

Labels as on the board: "adj. {h}" (only with an adjoining room), "beams {Height AFF}" (level only), "ridge {h}" (shown as "clg {h}" on flat ceilings). Re-draws on every inspector edit; no cut mark on the canvas | S05 Section row; board section SVG |

Slope from plate, ridge height and ridge position (not the stored pitch), so the profile closes. A level beam stops where its top meets the slope; one above the ridge is not drawn. Drawn scaled to fit the board's 330 × 140 box (height and width scaled apart). Beam fill uses --ez-amber-border (the board's wood tan has no token; rule C10) | S05 Section row; board section SVG |

By light level is unavailable until that tag has lumens and CU, LLF and the target are set | phase-05 row; catalog_item migration |
| EZD-509 | "Even grid" and grid by spacing | cols = ceil(√(n × long ÷ short)), rows = ceil(n ÷ cols); a short last row is centred. Half-spacing rule per direction. By spacing: floor(side ÷ spacing) per side, centred. Rooms that are not rectangles use the bounding box along the longest wall and drop points outside the outline; the preview shows the real count. Worked example: 8 cans in 20' × 16' → 4 × 2 at x = 2' 6", 7' 6", 12' 6", 17' 6" and y = 4' 0", 12' 0" (the board shows 4 × 2 with y at 4' and 12') | AC-EZD-509-2; board can circles |

HEIC is converted by the existing pin-photo and assistant path, because the AI image type takes only png, jpeg or webp | 05-screens.md#Q-0637; 03-data-model.md#Q-0513; ai/types.ts |

| EZD-607 | Rounding needed to reach 35,868 VA and 149.5 A | Each line is rounded to whole VA, half up, before summing. The demand line is 6,097.5, shown as 6,098. Unrounded, the total is 35,867.5 VA, which gives 149.4 A and fails the AC. Amps use exact half-up on integers: 35,868 × 10 / 240 = 1,494.5, which rounds to 149.5. A floating-point round of 149.45 can land on 149.4. Percent is whole (74.75% shows as 75%). Spare is 50.5 A | 09a table; board S06 |
| EZD-607 | Where the working copy keeps the calc inputs (the electrical_calc row is written only at issue) | On the panel object in model_json: `loadCalc` {method, case, floorAreaSqFt, loads[], overrides, largestMotorId}. Each `loads[]` entry has id, name, kind (fixed_appliance, dryer, range, heating, cooling, ev_charger, other), VA or volts and amps, motor, existing, confirmed, sourceObjectId | 03c electrical_calc and panel; 13b R3; 13a P3 |
| EZD-607 | Square footage "from rooms" when only a kitchen is drawn | Prefilled with the sum of the room areas and typed over for the whole dwelling; the typed value wins. Empty shows the S06 prompt. Room areas are measured to the inside finished face (Q-0559), while catalog NE2-134 (Unverified) wants outside dimensions | 09a; S06 Empty; NE2-134 |
| EZD-607 | Which fixtures make which lines | Dishwasher, disposal and water_heater make fixed-appliance lines; dryer makes the dryer line; range and cooktop make the range line. Refrigerator is left out (09a). Washer is left out: the example has a laundry line and no washer line. Microwave, heating, cooling and EV have no fixture kind, so they are `loads[]` entries | FIXTURE_KINDS; 09a |
| EZD-607 | Rating order | Catalog item rating, then the appliance default (EZD-614), then the calc error naming the input. Fixtures link to catalog items only with EZD-1602 (phase 31), so the catalog step is a pointer until then. Utility-point amps are not used as a rating | 09a; S09:54; EZD-1602 |
| EZD-607 | The 75% fixed-appliance rule, read literally | 75% applies when there are four or more fixed appliances and each is rated 500 VA or more; otherwise 100%. There is no hp field. Cooking, dryers, heating, cooling and EV are never in the group | 09a; NE4-009 |
| EZD-607 | Table 220.55: the spec gives only the "one range not over 12 kW, column C = 8,000" row | Superseded 2026-11-18 by Q-0761: column C for 1 to 5 appliances, Note 1 (over 12 kW up to 27 kW) and Note 4 (cooktop plus ovens) rows are in 09r; columns A and B are not used; more than 5 cooking appliances or a range over 27 kW gives the calc error naming the range line | 09a; 09r; 09-calculations-and-rules.md#Q-0761 |

| EZD-733 | How a tile layout is stored | New model type `tile_layout` (version stays 7): host is a room's floor (`roomId`) or one face of a wall (`wallId`, `left`/`right` side walking the wall start to end, and the set height the tile runs to); pattern, tile width and height, joint (0 = butted), status, and a start point = the lower-left corner of one whole tile. Floor start is a plan point; wall start is distance along the wall's line from its start point and height above the floor, so it follows the wall when it moves | phase-07 EZD-733 row; 09i Wall tile "to its set height"; Q-0559 |
| EZD-733 | New layout defaults | 12" x 24" tile, 1/8" joint, straight; floor start at the room outline's lower-left box corner; wall start at the wall's left end in the elevation at the floor; wall tile to 96" (the default wall height, Q-0558) | not in the spec; closest common sizes |
| EZD-733 | Pattern geometry | Straight: rows and columns one joint apart from the start; diagonal: that grid turned 45° about the start; herringbone: horizontal and vertical tiles in pairs (long side = the larger of width and height), turned so the zig-zag runs along the wall or across the plan page. A piece is a cut when it is smaller than a whole tile; cuts are drawn (shaded and dashed on the elevation; piece outlines on the plan), never counted or listed | S19 Tile pattern select; non-goals "Tile cut list (v2)" |
| EZD-733 | Which face the cabinet elevation shows and what covers the tile | The face of the run's host wall the run stands on: by the run start's side of the wall line, or, for a start on the line (the drawn finished face), the side away from the wall's thickness. Cabinets, windows and fixtures are drawn over the tile; openings are not cut out of it (3D and quantities stay in EZD-1614). The only elevation today is the cabinet run screen (EZD-404), so wall tile is edited there; a wall with no run gets its tile elevation with EZD-738's sheet | Q-0559; R24; phase-16 EZD-1614 row |
| EZD-733 | Start point editing and limits | Typed in the bar (from the wall's left end / room's left and bottom, and height) or, on the elevation, dragged by its round handle (to 1/16", never below the floor). More than 20,000 tiles over one area draws nothing and shows a note. A layout whose room or wall is deleted stays stored and draws nothing (omitted as host_not_found on the plan) | Q-0207; 11 Budgets |

| EZD-407 | Which fit rule | The ticket row's: gap = wall length − run total; green when the gap is from 0 to the base row fillers' scribe plus the Defaults "Cabinet fit" tolerance (0 while not set), both ends inclusive at the 1/64" comparison tolerance; amber otherwise. A run longer than its wall is never green, whatever the tolerance. The S04 Decisions line alone ("within tolerance") would flag the AC's run while the tolerance is unset | phase-04 EZD-407 row; AC-EZD-407-1; 09f Cabinet rules; 03g 1/64" |
| EZD-407 | Wall length | The host wall's end-to-end extent along the run (the elevation's wall), as the EZD-404 drawing uses. A run off every wall shows no chip (it has its red "Not on a wall" flag, EZD-410) | elevation.ts `wall`; S04 Validation |

Green text is board S04's. The scribe part shows even at 0" | S04 States "fit chip turns to warning with the difference"; board S04Cabinets |
| EZD-408 | Which line Auto-fill uses | The cabinet library and its line picker (EZD-403) are not built, so there is no active line: Auto-fill uses the seeded generic line (EZD-401, R23): base boxes B9 to B36 in the Defaults auto-fill increment (3"), filler F3. New boxes are type base, 24" × 34 1/2", B9–B24 "drawer + 1 door" hinged left, B27–B36 "drawer + 2 doors" hinged pair (library rows "Base, 1 door", "Base, 2 doors"; board B15); status New; no catalog item | phase-04 EZD-408; S04 library rows; assumptions EZD-401 seed row |
| EZD-408 | How the remainder goes into fillers | An F3 is held at each wall end; boxes fill from the left, widest first, until none fits. Whole increments left over widen the far wall-end filler (F6, F9); the part under one increment is that filler's scribe allowance, so wall − total equals the scribe and the fit chip (EZD-407) is green. 117 1/2": F3, B36, B36, B36, F6 with 1/2" scribe = 117", board S04's chip "Run 117" fits wall 117 1/2" · 1/2" scribe" | phase-04 EZD-408 "put the remainder into fillers at the wall ends"; AC-EZD-408-1; board S04Cabinets |
| EZD-408 | What is kept, and appliances and openings | Sink bases and appliance panels in the base row stay where they stand; the gap before each is filled the same way, with a filler of the exact rest beside it. A run's row cannot hold a hole, so placed floor-standing appliances (range, refrigerator, dishwasher, washer, dryer, water heater) on the run's side of the wall and openings on the host wall whose sill is under the 36" countertop top (doors, low windows) end the fill: Auto-fill fills the free stretch of wall the run stands in, with a wall-end filler against them, and the run start moves to that stretch's start. The other side of such an appliance takes its own run. The fit chip still compares with the whole wall | phase-04 EZD-408 "keep placed appliances, sink bases and openings"; 09f "fillers at walls and appliances"; EZD-402 run rows |
| EZD-408 | Upper row and the confirm step | Auto-fill fills the base row only; the upper row is left as it is. The result is drawn (plan strip, elevation, chain and fit chip) with a bar under the header; nothing changes until Confirm, which is one undo step. Cancel, Esc or any other model change drops it. The bar shows even on an empty run, as S04 "shows result for confirmation" | S04 Controls Auto-fill run |
| EZD-408 | Confirm bar wording (for Josh to confirm) | "Auto-fill result", "Confirm to replace the boxes in this run. Sink bases and appliance panels stay.", buttons "Confirm" and "Cancel" | S04 Controls Auto-fill run (no board text) |
| EZD-419 | How a run is "selected on Plan" when the plan does not draw cabinets | Cabinets are not drawn on the plan canvas yet (EZD-410 row), so the run is selected on the cabinet run screen, which keeps the Plan tab (EZD-404): the run name in the header is a button; a click selects the run and the inspector shows the run inspector. A box click still shows the box inspector, and no selection still shows its hint. Delete and Duplicate ignore a selected run | S03 Controls — inspector; assumptions.md EZD-404, EZD-410 |

| EZD-505 | What one stored beam object is | One `beam` object is a beam set on one room's ceiling (room id, a move shift, the S05 inspector fields); the beams are worked out from the room, never stored. | EZD-505 | "Below the ridge" | The beam top (Height AFF plus the beam height) must be at or under the ridge height; "at or above the wall plate" is Height AFF at or over the plate. | EZD-505 | The wall plate and an adjoining room's ceiling height | Plate = the lowest stored per-wall ceiling height, else the lowest height of the walls whose face lies on the room's outline. Adjoining room = a room on the same floor with an edge facing this room's edge across 12" or less (one wall), longest shared run first; its finished ceiling height is its plate | S05 Match "finished ceiling height of the adjoining room"; no spec value |
| EZD-505 | How many beams and where | Spacing: as many beams as whole spacings fit in the span; Count: the span shared out in equal parts; either way the set is centred, so the end gaps are half a spacing or more (20' at 4' 0" gives 5 beams, 2' 0" at each end, as on the board). Ridge direction from the ridge line, else the room's longest edge | board S05Ceiling "5 beams", "4' 0" o.c."; S05 Count "spacing is computed" |
| EZD-505 | Defaults of a new set | Placement from the ceiling type (vaulted: Level beams in vault; single slope: Follow slope; others: On flat ceiling); Match Adjoining room with its height when there is one, else Custom at the plate (8' 0" if unknown); 6" × 8" at 4' 0" o.c., wall to wall, Across ridge, Square to slope | board S05Ceiling values; mockup sample data is not a decision |

| EZD-505 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on a mockup) | "Count", "Length", "Wall to wall", "With ridge", "Corbel", "Follow slope", "On flat ceiling", "Custom"; warnings "Beams above the ridge" / "Level beams must sit below the ridge. Raise Height AFF."; "No adjoining room to match"; "Type a whole number from 1 to 200"; "The beams could not be changed"; "Click inside a room to place beams"; subtitle "{n} beams · {room name}" (board shows "5 beams · family room") | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

## EZD-412 (2026-11-20)
| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |

| EZD-412 | Where the countertop screen lives | Like the cabinet run screen (EZD-404): the Countertops (K) tool, after Cabinets on every viewer's rail and never paused, opens a screen over the plan drawn as one SVG (walls, dashed base cabinets, tops). Its tool bar shows Trace cabinets and Draw outline; Seam and Cutout come with EZD-413, the left panel and status bar with EZD-416, the inspector with EZD-414 to EZD-417. | EZD-412 | What Trace makes | One piece per stretch of counter-height base boxes in a run (a tall box splits a run, as the elevation's countertop). Front exposed at the Defaults Standard overhang; on a wall-hosted run the back is a wall edge at the Against wall overhang with a 4" splash; on a run off every wall all four sides are exposed at Standard. Each end: a wall just past it makes a wall edge (Against wall, 4" splash); other cabinets just past it make a joint (kind wall, no overhang, no splash, so not exposed); otherwise exposed at Standard. A joint that would run into another top stops at that top's edge: stretches with no butted end are topped first, so at an L corner the run that meets walls at both ends (or is open) keeps the corner whatever the model order, and the return stops at its front. Letters follow the model order. Name is the run name; thickness 3 cm; material not set | S04b Trace cabinets "with default overhangs"; S15d Default overhangs; board S04b "Quartz · 3 cm", "Backsplash 4" · same", A "141 1/2 × 25 1/2", B "63 × 25 1/2"; EZD-411 rows |
| EZD-412 | Pressing Trace again | Adds tops only for stretches not already under a top; labels continue with the next free letters (A … Z, AA …). Nothing is replaced | S04b; 03c label A/B… |
| EZD-412 | A run off every wall: which side is the front | The left side of the run axis (model y up), the side a hosted run's front takes when its wall is drawn along the axis with its thickness to the right. Only the edge order depends on it; all four sides are exposed | cabinets/elevation.ts wallFace |
| EZD-412 | A drawn outline's edges | Every edge exposed with 0 overhang (a custom shape has no cabinets under it to measure from); edges are set afterwards with EZD-414. Thickness 3 cm. A shape that crosses itself or has no area is not made | S04b Draw outline |
| EZD-412 | Draw outline snaps and typed lengths | A corner of a base cabinet or another top within 10 px; otherwise from the last corner the direction to 15° and the length to the Defaults countertop increment (1/2" when not read); the first corner to the increment grid. A typed length places the next corner that far toward the pointer. A click on the first corner closes the shape. A dragged corner snaps to a corner, else to the increment grid | S04b "Snaps as in 03"; S15d Countertop increment 1/2" |
| EZD-412 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on the board) | Hint with no cabinets: "Add cabinets to trace tops from them, or draw an outline."; while drawing: "Click each corner, or type a length. Click the first corner to finish."; typed field label "Length"; rail tooltip "Countertops". "Trace cabinets" and "Draw outline" are board text | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

## EZD-413 (2026-11-20)

| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EZD-413 | Where seams and cutouts are stored | On the piece, not as new model types: a seam edge carries a `seamId` shared by the two pieces on either side (kind wall, no overhang, no splash, no profile, as Trace's joints), so a seam counts and draws once; `cutouts` is a list on the piece (id, sink or cooktop, center, width direction, W, D, fixture id). Both fields are optional, so pieces saved before them still parse and the schema version stays | 03c; 03f "Countertop pieces, seams, cutouts"; S04b footer "pieces, seams" |
| EZD-413 | How a click places a seam | The seam runs square to the side of the piece nearest the click, through the click (its place along that side rounded to the Defaults countertop increment), across to the first side it meets. A seam that would not cut the piece in two, or that runs through a cutout, is not made | S04b "Click across a piece to split it" |
| EZD-413 | Which part keeps the label and how pieces relabel | The split piece keeps its id as the larger part; the smaller part is a new piece. Every piece on the floor is lettered again A, B, C … in its old label order with the two parts side by side, larger first (board: back A 141 1/2, return B 63, island C). Both parts keep the name, material and thickness. One undo step | S04b "Pieces relabel (A, B, C)"; board S04bCountertops |
| EZD-413 | Cutout size and place | Over a placed sink or cooktop fixture: its center, direction and W × D, linked to it (fixture id kept for EZD-1602's catalog cutout size). On a bare top: the chosen kind (Sink or Cooktop) and the typed W × D at the click, center on the increment grid, width along the nearest side. Starting sizes until typed: sink 30" × 16", cooktop 30" × 21". A cutout must lie wholly on its top. Clicking a placed cutout picks it; Enter in W or D sets the picked cutout's size about its center. Cutouts do not change area or exposed edge (board totals 56.1 sq ft count outlines only) | phase-04 EZD-413 row; S04b Cutout; board totals |
| EZD-413 | Delete of a seam or cutout | Not built here: S04b's Delete / Backspace row has no ticket in phase 4's list for seams and cutouts; Undo removes one just placed | S04b Controls; phase-04 |
| EZD-413 | Icons | Seam `view_column_2`, Cutout `crop_square` from the icon set (the board's scissors and corner glyphs are not in it) | board S04bCountertops tool bar |
| EZD-413 | Wording for Josh to confirm (not on the board) | Hints "Click across a piece to split it." and "Click a top to place a cutout. Over a sink or cooktop it takes that size."; kind buttons "Sink", "Cooktop" (group "Cutout type"); fields "W", "D"; cutout label "COOKTOP". "Seam", "Cutout", "SEAM", "SINK" are board text | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

Wording for Josh to confirm: "Electrical", "Plumbing", "Mechanical", "Reflected ceiling plan", and the default unticked for Reflected ceiling plan | Q-0764; Q-0779; CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

Wording for Josh to confirm: "Ceiling work is drawn but Reflected ceiling plan is not ticked." and "Reflected ceiling plan is ticked but no ceiling work is drawn." | Q-0779; Q-0764; S05 |

The cover index (EZD-728) and "sheet x of y" count only the sheets in the set. | EZD-743 | Duct run data, drawing and wording | A duct run is a model object on the Mechanical layer: kind supply or return, an ordered list of points (straight segments, drawn like the wall tool with typed lengths, Q-0561), a size typed as a round diameter (8") or width x height (14" x 8"), and the ids of the equipment and register at its ends; an end follows its register or equipment when that moves. Drawn as a double line at the duct width (round ducts at their diameter) in the hidden-line weight on plan, with the size label centred on the longest segment. No sizing or air-flow check (Q-0780). EZD-731 built equipment and registers but no ducts (its AC named only those). Wording for Josh to confirm: "Duct run", "Supply", "Return", "Size", "Round", "Rectangular" | Q-0780; R-012 recommendation 5; EZD-731 assumption row "Ducts and the bath fan" |

When placed they print on A6.0 with their RCP symbols; placing one is ceiling work for the Reflected ceiling plan box (Q-0779) | Q-0780; Q-0779; S05 HVAC diffuser or return |

Other mechanical rules are unaffected: M-023, M-072 and M-087 in EZD-624/625 are already Reminders; M-053, M-066 and M-071 (dryer duct length, bath fan duct, exhaust rates) are code checks on typed values, not load or sizing calculations | Q-0780; code-rules.json EN-037 to EN-039, M-023, M-072, M-087 |
| EZD-1401 | How the open project's data is kept for offline | The editor's model load (GET /api/projects/<id>/model) is stored by the page in the worker's project cache (ezdraft-projects) each time it loads online; Chromium sends /api/* straight to the network (EZD-134 static routes), so a worker route could never answer it. With no network the load is answered from that copy, read-only (the copy says the viewer cannot edit: no lock, no autosave, no drawing tools); offline editing is EZD-1403. The worker's budget trim and sign-out cover the copy. Sheet images and 400 px / 1600 px render copies are not built yet; their tickets store them the same way | 11 Offline; phase-14.md EZD-1401 row; sw-rules.mjs NETWORK_ONLY_PATHS |

Green offline_pin "Saved on this device for offline use"; spinner "Saving for offline use…"; amber cloud_off with the count "Offline. Changes waiting to sync: {n}" (hover and screen-reader text). Nothing shows before the first store or after a failed store | S17 Offline status icon; board S17Field; CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

A phone never opens the editor ("Editing is done on an iPad or a computer"), so the phone's offline open is EZD-1404's field pack test | Q-0338; AC-EZD-1401-2 ("the field pack is covered in EZD-1404"); field.device.editor_on_phone |

To make room (EZD-215 precedent), two parts shown only after a user action now load on that action: the review list (on the first press of the review pill, lazy/review-list) and the beam spacing pill (when the Ceiling or Both view first shows, lazy/beam-spacing). The route measures 499,716 bytes. The limit is unchanged | 11 Budgets; 11 rule 7; EZD-120c B11; EZD-215 row |
